It reads one CSV file, infers each column's type, reports counts, extremes, and\nfor numeric columns the sum, mean, and median, and can list the most frequent values of one column.\nThere is no persistence and no network I/O.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`:\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Go 1.22 or newer | The whole application; standard library only, no third-party modules. |\n| go test and go vet | The test runner behind `bin/test.sh`. |\n| CSV (RFC 4180 via `encoding/csv`) | Input reading and output writing. |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh` and the supplied grader `sources/run.sh`. |\n\n## Module Layout\n\n```text\ngo.mod                    # module path, go 1.22 or newer, no require lines\ncmd/csvstat/main.go       # package main; calls internal/cli.Run and passes the code to os.Exit\ninternal/cli/             # argument parsing, usage text, dispatch, exit-code mapping\ninternal/csvio/           # file opening, CSV reading, trimming, validation\ninternal/profile/         # null rules, type inference, counts, statistics, frequencies\ninternal/decimal/         # exact arithmetic with math/big and two-decimal formatting\nbin/test.sh               # POSIX sh: go vet ./... then go test ./...\n```\n\n`internal/cli.Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int` is the single entry point. Only\n`cmd/csvstat/main.go` calls `os.Exit`. Behavior is tested with `bytes.Buffer`.\n\n## Module Ownership\n\n| Boundary | Owning package | Low-level APIs it may access |\n|---|---|---|\n| Command-line arguments, usage text, exit codes | `internal/cli` | `flag`, `io` |\n| File access and CSV parsing | `internal/csvio` | `os`, `encoding/csv`, `strings` |\n| Column semantics (null, type, counts, stats, frequency) | `internal/profile` | `time`, `sort`, `bytes` |\n| Exact numbers and rounding | `internal/decimal` | `math/big` |\n| Persistence, configuration files, external services | none | not applicable |\n\n## Exit Codes\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | Success. |\n| 1 | The file cannot be opened or read. |\n| 2 | Usage error. |\n| 3 | Rejected input: empty file, empty or repeated header name, ragged record, CSV syntax error, or a column name absent from the header. |\n\nA failing command writes nothing to standard output and one diagnostic line to standard error.\nOutput is buffered until validation completes.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data file and never embeds a\n  statistic, type, or value list.\n- Sums, means, and medians are exact (`math/big`) and rounded once, half away from zero, at output.\n- Input bytes are compared as bytes; a byte-order mark is not stripped.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC arch-module-builds ===\nIntent: The application root is a Go module whose main package at ./cmd/csvstat builds with go build.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nassert Path(\"go.mod\").is_file()\nassert Path(\"cmd/csvstat/main.go\").is_file()\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    target = str(Path(tmp) / \"csvstat\")\n    result = subprocess.run([\"go\", \"build\", \"-o\", target, \"./cmd/csvstat\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\n    assert result.returncode == 0\n    assert Path(target).is_file()\n=== END AC arch-module-builds ===\n\n=== AC arch-stdlib-only ===\nIntent: The module declares no third-party requirement and the main package depends only on this module and the standard library.\nRequires: executable=go; Options precede exactly one file.\n\n| Option | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `--column <name>` | Profile only the named column. |\n| `--freq <name>` | List the most frequent values of the named column. |\n| `--top <n>` | With `--freq`, list at most n values; default 5. |\n| `--help` | Write usage text to standard output and exit 0. |\n\n## Behavior\n\n1. Parsing yields a typed request: mode (profile or frequency), column name, frequency name, top\n   count, and file path.\n2. `--help` writes usage text naming both command forms and every option to standard output and\n   returns 0, writing nothing to standard error.\n3. Profile and frequency modes are dispatched through small interfaces held by `Run`, so later\n   stories plug in without changing parsing.\n4. Names given to `--column` and `--freq` are kept exactly as given; they are matched later,\n   exactly and case-sensitively, against trimmed header names.\n5. Usage-error exit mapping is owned by the usage-errors story; this story only has to produce the\n   request for a well-formed command line.\n\n## Required Go Tests\n\nThe build adds these tests in `internal/cli` (names are exact): `TestParseHelp`,\n`TestParseColumnForm`, `TestParseFreqForm`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC cliparse-help-exits-zero ===\nIntent: csvstat --help exits 0 and writes usage naming the command and every option to standard output.\n\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"--help\")\nprint(out)\nprint(err)\nassert code == 0\nfor token in [\"csvstat\", \"--column\", \"--freq\", \"--top\"]:\n    assert token in out, token\n=== END AC cliparse-help-exits-zero ===\n\n=== AC cliparse-help-test ===\nIntent: The package test for the help form passes.\nRequires: executable=go; only `cmd/csvstat/main.go` does.\n- The flag set writes its own diagnostics to standard error, never standard output.\n- Standard library only.\n\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CsvReading.md ===\n# FEATURE: CSV Reading and Profile Output\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Read a well-formed CSV file and write the profile header and one row per column in file order. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CliParsing.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | profile output header and rows |\n| Consumes    | csvstat [--column <name>] <file> |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`internal/csvio` opens the file and reads it with `encoding/csv` as RFC 4180 defines it; the first\nrecord is the header. The profile mode writes CSV to standard output with `\\n` line endings.\n\n## Behavior\n\n1. The reader sets `FieldsPerRecord = -1` so field-count validation is done by the application,\n   which is owned by the rejection story.\n2. Standard output starts with the header `column,type,rows,nulls,distinct,min,max,sum,mean,median`,\n   then one row per column in file order. Each row has exactly ten fields; the first is the column\n   name. The remaining fields are produced by the later counts, types, and statistics stories; this\n   story establishes the row structure.\n3. A quoted field containing a comma is read as one value. A field containing a comma, a double\n   quote, or a newline is quoted on output by the `encoding/csv` writer.\n4. A file with a header and no data records succeeds with one row per header column.\n5. Bytes are compared as bytes: a byte-order mark is not stripped and the file is not rejected for\n   invalid UTF-8.\n6. Output is written to a buffer and copied to standard output only after reading succeeds.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC csvread-header-and-columns ===\nIntent: Profiling a well-formed file exits 0 and writes the declared header then one ten-field row per input column in file order.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\npath = \"sources/employees.csv\"\nwith open(path, newline=\"\") as handle:\n    input_header = next(csv.reader(handle))\ndeclared = \"column,type,rows,nulls,distinct,min,max,sum,mean,median\".split(\",\")\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(path)\nprint(out)\nprint(err)\nassert code == 0\nrows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\nassert rows[0] == declared\nassert [row[0] for row in rows[1:]] == input_header\nfor row in rows:\n    assert len(row) == len(declared)\n=== END AC csvread-header-and-columns ===\n\n=== AC csvread-line-endings ===\nIntent: Profile output uses newline line endings only.\n\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"sources/orders.csv\")\nassert code == 0\nassert \"\\r\" not in out\nassert out.endswith(\"\\n\")\n=== END AC csvread-line-endings ===\n\n=== AC csvread-quoted-comma ===\nIntent: A quoted field containing a comma is one value, so the file keeps its declared column count.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nnames = [\"a\", \"b\"]\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    target = Path(tmp) / \"quoted.csv\"\n    target.write_text(names[0] + \",\" + names[1] + \"\\n\" + '\"x, y\",2\\n')\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(str(target))\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 0\n    rows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\n    assert [row[0] for row in rows[1:]] == names\n=== END AC csvread-quoted-comma ===\n\n=== AC csvread-header-only ===\nIntent: A file with a header and no data records succeeds with one row per header column.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\npath = \"sources/header_only.csv\"\nwith open(path, newline=\"\") as handle:\n    input_header = next(csv.reader(handle))\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(path)\nprint(out)\nprint(err)\nassert code == 0\nrows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\nassert [row[0] for row in rows[1:]] == input_header\n=== END AC csvread-header-only ===\n\n=== AC csvread-bom-kept ===\nIntent: A byte-order mark is not stripped from the first header name.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nbom = \"\\ufeff\"\nfirst = \"a\"\nsecond = \"b\"\npayload = (bom + first + \",\" + second + \"\\n1,2\\n\").encode(\"utf-8\")\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    target = bom + first\n    assert rows[2][0] == second\n=== END AC csvread-bom-kept ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data library only.\n\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-ValueTrimming.md ===\n# FEATURE: Value Trimming\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Trim leading and trailing spaces from every header name and every value before any other rule applies. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CsvReading.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | Only the space character is trimmed (`strings.Trim(s, \" \")`); tabs and other whitespace remain.\n2. Header names are trimmed before the empty-name and repeated-name checks.\n3. Values are trimmed before the null test, so a value of spaces only is empty and therefore null.\n4. Column names written in the profile output are the trimmed names.\n\n## Required Go Tests\n\nThe build adds these tests in `internal/csvio` (names are exact): `TestTrimHeaderNames`,\n`TestTrimValues`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC trim-header-names ===\nIntent: Padded header names are written trimmed in the profile output.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nnames = [\"alpha\", \"beta\"]\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    target = Path(tmp) / \"padded.csv\"\n    target.write_text(\"  \" + names[0] + \" , \" + names[1] + \"  \\n1,2\\n\")\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(str(target))\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 0\n    rows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\n    assert [row[0] for row in rows[1:]] == names\n=== END AC trim-header-names ===\n\n=== AC trim-only-spaces ===\nIntent: Only spaces are trimmed; Rejection exits 3, writes nothing to\nstandard output, and writes one line to standard error naming the file and, for a record problem,\nits line number.\n\n## Behavior\n\n1. An empty file, or a file with no records at all, is rejected.\n2. A header name that is empty after trimming, or repeated after trimming, is rejected.\n3. A record whose field count differs from the header is rejected, naming its line number.\n4. A syntax error reported by `encoding/csv`, such as a bare quote in an unquoted field, is\n   rejected with exit 3 and names the file and line number.\n5. All output is buffered; nothing reaches standard output unless validation of the whole file\n   succeeded.\n6. A header-only file is valid and is not rejected.\n\n## Required Go Tests\n\nThe build adds these tests in `internal/csvio` (names are exact): `TestRejectEmptyFile`,\n`TestRejectBadHeader`, `TestRejectRaggedRecord`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC reject-ragged-supplied ===\nIntent: A record with a different field count than the header exits 3 with nothing on standard output and \"\"\nassert path in err\n=== END AC reject-ragged-supplied ===\n\n=== AC reject-empty-file ===\nIntent: A zero-byte file and a file holding only a blank line both exit 3 with nothing on standard code == 3\n        assert out == \"\"\n=== END AC reject-empty-file ===\n\n=== AC reject-bad-header-names ===\nIntent: An empty header name, a repeated header name, and a name repeated only after cli_runner(str(target))\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 3\n    assert out == \"\"\n    assert str(target) in err\n=== END AC reject-short-record ===\n\n=== AC reject-csv-syntax-error ===\nIntent: A bare quote inside an unquoted field is a CSV syntax error that exits 3 with nothing on standard output.\n\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom cli_runner(str(target))\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 3\n    assert out == \"\"\n    assert str(target) in err\n=== END AC reject-csv-syntax-error ===\n\n=== AC BEGIN ARTIFACT ARCHITECTURE.md ===\n# ARCHITECTURE: CsvStat\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Module layout, package ownership, and boundaries of the csvstat command-line CSV column profiler. |\n| Depends On  | None |\n| Provides    | go.mod, cmd/csvstat, internal/cli.Run |\n| Consumes    | None |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nCsvStat is a single-binary command-line profiler written in Go 1.22 or newer using only the\nstandard library. It reads one CSV file, infers each column's type, reports counts, extremes, and\nfor numeric columns the sum, mean, and median, and can list the most frequent values of one column.\nThere is no persistence and no network I/O.\n\n## Technology Stack\n\nDerived from `TECHNOLOGY_STACK.md`:\n\n| Technology | Where it applies |\n|---|---|\n| Go 1.22 or newer | The whole application; standard library only, no third-party modules. |\n| go test and go vet | The test runner behind `bin/test.sh`. |\n| CSV (RFC 4180 via `encoding/csv`) | Input reading and output writing. |\n| POSIX sh | `bin/test.sh` and the supplied grader `sources/run.sh`. |\n\n## Module Layout\n\n```text\ngo.mod                    # module path, go 1.22 or newer, no require lines\ncmd/csvstat/main.go       # package main; calls internal/cli.Run and passes the code to os.Exit\ninternal/cli/             # argument parsing, usage text, dispatch, exit-code mapping\ninternal/csvio/           # file opening, CSV reading, trimming, validation\ninternal/profile/         # null rules, type inference, counts, statistics, frequencies\ninternal/decimal/         # exact arithmetic with math/big and two-decimal formatting\nbin/test.sh               # POSIX sh: go vet ./... then go test ./...\n```\n\n`internal/cli.Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int` is the single entry point. Only\n`cmd/csvstat/main.go` calls `os.Exit`. Behavior is tested with `bytes.Buffer`.\n\n## Module Ownership\n\n| Boundary | Owning package | Low-level APIs it may access |\n|---|---|---|\n| Command-line arguments, usage text, exit codes | `internal/cli` | `flag`, `io` |\n| File access and CSV parsing | `internal/csvio` | `os`, `encoding/csv`, `strings` |\n| Column semantics (null, type, counts, stats, frequency) | `internal/profile` | `time`, `sort`, `bytes` |\n| Exact numbers and rounding | `internal/decimal` | `math/big` |\n| Persistence, configuration files, external services | none | not applicable |\n\n## Exit Codes\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| 0 | Success. |\n| 1 | The file cannot be opened or read. |\n| 2 | Usage error. |\n| 3 | Rejected input: empty file, empty or repeated header name, ragged record, CSV syntax error, or a column name absent from the header. |\n\nA failing command writes nothing to standard output and one diagnostic line to standard error.\nOutput is buffered until validation completes.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data file and never embeds a\n  statistic, type, or value list.\n- Sums, means, and medians are exact (`math/big`) and rounded once, half away from zero, at output.\n- Input bytes are compared as bytes; a byte-order mark is not stripped.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC arch-module-builds ===\nIntent: The application root is a Go module whose main package at ./cmd/csvstat builds with go build.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nassert Path(\"go.mod\").is_file()\nassert Path(\"cmd/csvstat/main.go\").is_file()\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    target = str(Path(tmp) / \"csvstat\")\n    result = subprocess.run([\"go\", \"build\", \"-o\", target, \"./cmd/csvstat\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\n    print(result.stdout)\n    print(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\n    assert result.returncode == 0\n    assert Path(target).is_file()\n=== END AC arch-module-builds ===\n\n=== AC arch-stdlib-only ===\nIntent: The module declares no third-party requirement and the main package depends only on this module and the standard library.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\ngo_mod = Path(\"go.mod\").read_text()\nmodule_line = [ln for ln in go_mod.splitlines() if ln.startswith(\"module \")]\nassert len(module_line) == 1\nmodule_path = module_line[0].split()[1]\nassert \"require\" not in go_mod\nresult = subprocess.run(\n    [\"go\", \"list\", \"-deps\", \"-f\", \"{{if not .Standard}}{{.ImportPath}}{{end}}\", \"./cmd/csvstat\"],\n    capture_output=True, text=True,\n)\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == 0\nfor line in result.stdout.split():\n    assert line.startswith(module_path), line\n=== END AC arch-stdlib-only ===\n\n=== AC arch-vet-clean ===\nIntent: The foundation tree passes go vet.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"go\", \"vet\", \"./...\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr, file=sys.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == 0\n=== END AC arch-vet-clean ===\n\n=== AC arch-only-main-exits ===\nIntent: Only the main package calls os.Exit and the internal packages exist as declared.\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nassert Path(\"internal/cli\").is_dir()\nexit_call = \"os.\" + \"Exit(\"\nfor path in Path(\"internal\").rglob(\"*.go\"):\n    if path.name.endswith(\"_test.go\"):\n        continue\n    assert exit_call not in path.read_text(), str(path)\nassert exit_call in Path(\"cmd/csvstat/main.go\").read_text()\n=== END AC arch-only-main-exits ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Standard library only; no third-party module may appear in `go.mod`.\n- Do not modify, rewrite, regenerate, or substitute any file under `sources/`.\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or a supplied data file and never hardcodes a statistic, type, or value list.\n- No database and no network access.\n\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CliParsing.md ===\n# FEATURE: Command Line Parsing\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Accept the two csvstat command forms and the --help text, producing a typed request for dispatch. |\n| Depends On  | ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | csvstat [--column <name>] <file>, csvstat --freq <name> [--top <n>] <file>, csvstat --help |\n| Consumes    | internal/cli.Run |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`internal/cli` parses arguments with a local `flag.FlagSet` created with `flag.ContinueOnError`,\nwhose output is directed to standard error. Options precede exactly one file.\n\n| Option | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `--column <name>` | Profile only the named column. |\n| `--freq <name>` | List the most frequent values of the named column. |\n| `--top <n>` | With `--freq`, list at most n values; default 5. |\n| `--help` | Write usage text to standard output and exit 0. |\n\n## Behavior\n\n1. Parsing yields a typed request: mode (profile or frequency), column name, frequency name, top\n   count, and file path.\n2. `--help` writes usage text naming both command forms and every option to standard output and\n   returns 0, writing nothing to standard error.\n3. Profile and frequency modes are dispatched through small interfaces held by `Run`, so later\n   stories plug in without changing parsing.\n4. Names given to `--column` and `--freq` are kept exactly as given; they are matched later,\n   exactly and case-sensitively, against trimmed header names.\n5. Usage-error exit mapping is owned by the usage-errors story; this story only has to produce the\n   request for a well-formed command line.\n\n## Required Go Tests\n\nThe build adds these tests in `internal/cli` (names are exact): `TestParseHelp`,\n`TestParseColumnForm`, `TestParseFreqForm`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC cliparse-help-exits-zero ===\nIntent: csvstat --help exits 0 and writes usage naming the command and every option to standard output.\n\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"--help\")\nprint(out)\nprint(err)\nassert code == 0\nfor token in [\"csvstat\", \"--column\", \"--freq\", \"--top\"]:\n    assert token in out, token\n=== END AC cliparse-help-exits-zero ===\n\n=== AC cliparse-help-test ===\nIntent: The package test for the help form passes.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", \"TestParseHelp\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC cliparse-help-test ===\n\n=== AC cliparse-column-form-test ===\nIntent: Parsing the --column form yields a profile request with the named column and the file path.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", \"TestParseColumnForm\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC cliparse-column-form-test ===\n\n=== AC cliparse-freq-form-test ===\nIntent: Parsing the --freq form yields a frequency request with the name, default top of 5, and the file path.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/cli\", \"TestParseFreqForm\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC cliparse-freq-form-test ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- `Run` never calls `os.Exit`; only `cmd/csvstat/main.go` does.\n- The flag set writes its own diagnostics to standard error, never standard output.\n- Standard library only.\n\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-CsvReading.md ===\n# FEATURE: CSV Reading and Profile Output\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Read a well-formed CSV file and write the profile header and one row per column in file order. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CliParsing.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | profile output header and rows |\n| Consumes    | csvstat [--column <name>] <file> |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\n`internal/csvio` opens the file and reads it with `encoding/csv` as RFC 4180 defines it; the first\nrecord is the header. The profile mode writes CSV to standard output with `\\n` line endings.\n\n## Behavior\n\n1. The reader sets `FieldsPerRecord = -1` so field-count validation is done by the application,\n   which is owned by the rejection story.\n2. Standard output starts with the header `column,type,rows,nulls,distinct,min,max,sum,mean,median`,\n   then one row per column in file order. Each row has exactly ten fields; the first is the column\n   name. The remaining fields are produced by the later counts, types, and statistics stories; this\n   story establishes the row structure.\n3. A quoted field containing a comma is read as one value. A field containing a comma, a double\n   quote, or a newline is quoted on output by the `encoding/csv` writer.\n4. A file with a header and no data records succeeds with one row per header column.\n5. Bytes are compared as bytes: a byte-order mark is not stripped and the file is not rejected for\n   invalid UTF-8.\n6. Output is written to a buffer and copied to standard output only after reading succeeds.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC csvread-header-and-columns ===\nIntent: Profiling a well-formed file exits 0 and writes the declared header then one ten-field row per input column in file order.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\npath = \"sources/employees.csv\"\nwith open(path, newline=\"\") as handle:\n    input_header = next(csv.reader(handle))\ndeclared = \"column,type,rows,nulls,distinct,min,max,sum,mean,median\".split(\",\")\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(path)\nprint(out)\nprint(err)\nassert code == 0\nrows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\nassert rows[0] == declared\nassert [row[0] for row in rows[1:]] == input_header\nfor row in rows:\n    assert len(row) == len(declared)\n=== END AC csvread-header-and-columns ===\n\n=== AC csvread-line-endings ===\nIntent: Profile output uses newline line endings only.\n\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(\"sources/orders.csv\")\nassert code == 0\nassert \"\\r\" not in out\nassert out.endswith(\"\\n\")\n=== END AC csvread-line-endings ===\n\n=== AC csvread-quoted-comma ===\nIntent: A quoted field containing a comma is one value, so the file keeps its declared column count.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nnames = [\"a\", \"b\"]\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    target = Path(tmp) / \"quoted.csv\"\n    target.write_text(names[0] + \",\" + names[1] + \"\\n\" + '\"x, y\",2\\n')\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(str(target))\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 0\n    rows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\n    assert [row[0] for row in rows[1:]] == names\n=== END AC csvread-quoted-comma ===\n\n=== AC csvread-header-only ===\nIntent: A file with a header and no data records succeeds with one row per header column.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\npath = \"sources/header_only.csv\"\nwith open(path, newline=\"\") as handle:\n    input_header = next(csv.reader(handle))\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(path)\nprint(out)\nprint(err)\nassert code == 0\nrows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\nassert [row[0] for row in rows[1:]] == input_header\n=== END AC csvread-header-only ===\n\n=== AC csvread-bom-kept ===\nIntent: A byte-order mark is not stripped from the first header name.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nbom = \"\\ufeff\"\nfirst = \"a\"\nsecond = \"b\"\npayload = (bom + first + \",\" + second + \"\\n1,2\\n\").encode(\"utf-8\")\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    target = Path(tmp) / \"bom.csv\"\n    target.write_bytes(payload)\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(str(target))\n    assert code == 0\n    rows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\n    assert rows[1][0] == bom + first\n    assert rows[2][0] == second\n=== END AC csvread-bom-kept ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Application code never reads `sources/run.sh` or any supplied data file by name.\n- A byte-order mark is not stripped and invalid UTF-8 is not a rejection reason.\n- Standard output receives nothing until the whole file has been read and validated.\n- Standard library only.\n\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-ValueTrimming.md ===\n# FEATURE: Value Trimming\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Trim leading and trailing spaces from every header name and every value before any other rule applies. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CsvReading.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | trimmed header names and values |\n| Consumes    | profile output header and rows |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nLeading and trailing spaces are removed from every header name and every value before null, type,\ndistinct, and output rules apply. Trimming is done once, in `internal/csvio`, so every later rule\nsees trimmed text.\n\n## Behavior\n\n1. Only the space character is trimmed (`strings.Trim(s, \" \")`); tabs and other whitespace remain.\n2. Header names are trimmed before the empty-name and repeated-name checks.\n3. Values are trimmed before the null test, so a value of spaces only is empty and therefore null.\n4. Column names written in the profile output are the trimmed names.\n\n## Required Go Tests\n\nThe build adds these tests in `internal/csvio` (names are exact): `TestTrimHeaderNames`,\n`TestTrimValues`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC trim-header-names ===\nIntent: Padded header names are written trimmed in the profile output.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nnames = [\"alpha\", \"beta\"]\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    target = Path(tmp) / \"padded.csv\"\n    target.write_text(\"  \" + names[0] + \" , \" + names[1] + \"  \\n1,2\\n\")\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(str(target))\n    print(out)\n    print(err)\n    assert code == 0\n    rows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\n    assert [row[0] for row in rows[1:]] == names\n=== END AC trim-header-names ===\n\n=== AC trim-only-spaces ===\nIntent: Only spaces are trimmed; a trailing tab stays part of the header name.\n\nimport csv\nimport io\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nname = \"alpha\\t\"\nother = \"beta\"\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    target = Path(tmp) / \"tab.csv\"\n    target.write_text(\" \" + name + \" ,\" + other + \"\\n1,2\\n\")\n    code, out, err = cli_runner(str(target))\n    assert code == 0\n    rows = list(csv.reader(io.StringIO(out)))\n    assert rows[1][0] == name\n    assert rows[2][0] == other\n=== END AC trim-only-spaces ===\n\n=== AC trim-header-test ===\nIntent: The package test for header trimming passes.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestTrimHeaderNames\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC trim-header-test ===\n\n=== AC trim-values-test ===\nIntent: The package test for value trimming passes.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nfrom drydock_go import run_test\n\nresult = run_test(\"./internal/csvio\", \"TestTrimValues\")\nassert result.passed, (result.status, result.stdout, result.stderr)\n=== END AC trim-values-test ===\n\n## User Acceptance\n\n- None.\n\n## Guardrails\n\n- Tabs, non-breaking spaces, and other whitespace are not trimmed.\n- Trimming happens before every other rule; no later package re-trims or sees untrimmed text.\n- Standard library only.\n\n=== END ARTIFACT ===\n=== BEGIN ARTIFACT FEATURE-RejectMalformed.md ===\n# FEATURE: Reject Malformed Input\n\n| Field       | Value |\n|-------------|-------|\n| Version     | 20261025 V1 |\n| Description | Reject empty files, bad header names, ragged records, and CSV syntax errors with exit code 3 and nothing on standard output. |\n| Depends On  | FEATURE-CsvReading.md, ARCHITECTURE.md |\n| Provides    | exit code 3 for rejected input |\n| Consumes    | internal/csvio |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nMalformed input is rejected before any output is produced. Rejection exits 3, writes nothing to\nstandard output, and writes one line to standard error naming the file and, for a record problem,\nits line number.\n\n## Behavior\n\n1. An empty file, or a file with no records at all, is rejected.\n2. A header name that is empty after trimming, or repeated after trimming, is rejected.\n3. A record whose field count differs from the header is rejected, naming its line number.\n4. A syntax error reported by `encoding/csv`, such as a bare quote in an unquoted field, is\n   rejected with exit 3 and names the file and line number.\n5. All output is buffered; nothing reaches standard output unless validation of the whole file\n   succeeded.\n6. A header-only file is valid and is not rejected.\n\n## Required Go Tests\n\nThe build adds these tests in `internal/csvio` (names are exact): `TestRejectEmptyFile`,\n`TestRejectBadHeader`, `TestRejectRaggedRecord`.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC reject-ragged-supplied ===\nIntent: A record with a different field count than the header exits 3 with nothing on standard output and the file named on standard error.\n\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\npath = \"sources/ragged.csv\"\ncode, out, err = cli_runner(path)\nprint(out)\nprint(err)\nassert code == 3\nassert out == \"\"\nassert path in err\n=== END AC reject-ragged-supplied ===\n\n=== AC reject-empty-file ===\nIntent: A zero-byte file and a file holding only a blank line both exit 3 with nothing on standard output.\n\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    for index, payload in enumerate([b\"\", b\"\\n\"]):\n        target = Path(tmp) / (\"empty\" + str(index) + \".csv\")\n        target.write_bytes(payload)\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(str(target))\n        print(out)\n        print(err)\n        assert code == 3\n        assert out == \"\"\n=== END AC reject-empty-file ===\n\n=== AC reject-bad-header-names ===\nIntent: An empty header name, a repeated header name, and a name repeated only after trimming each exit 3 with nothing on standard output.\n\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\ncases = [\",b\\n1,2\\n\", \"a,a\\n1,2\\n\", \"a, a\\n1,2\\n\", \"a, \\n1,2\\n\"]\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    for index, text in enumerate(cases):\n        target = Path(tmp) / (\"header\" + str(index) + \".csv\")\n        target.write_text(text)\n        code, out, err = cli_runner(str(target))\n        print(out)\n        print(err)\n        assert code == 3, text\n        assert out == \"\"\n=== END AC reject-bad-header-names ===\n\n=== AC reject-short-record ===\nIntent: A record with fewer fields than the header exits 3 with nothing on standard output.\n\nimport tempfile\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom drydock_go import cli_runner\n\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    target = Path(tmp) / \"short.csv\"\n    target.write_text(\"a,b\\n1\\n\")\n    code, out, err =
